Well, I guess I came in because... for the last few weeks I’ve barely had an appetite. It's weird because usually after a shift, I'm starving. Yeah, it's not just the food. I used to really look forward to my morning runs, playing my guitar, cooking dinner... but lately, I keep skipping all of it. They just feel like chores that I can't get myself to care about anymore.

Yeah, I guess you could say that. Most days I just feel like I'm carrying around this heavy, flat feeling. Even when I'm home with the wife and kids, or just sitting in the break room at work, I feel like I'm just... not really there. 

Speaking of the break room, they finally fixed the vending machine. For like three months, it would just eat your quarters if you tried to buy the peanut butter crackers. I must have lost at least five bucks to that thing. The maintenance guy, Frank—he's this older guy, always wears these suspenders with tools hanging off them—he finally got in there and fixed it. Said a candy bar wrapper got jammed in the coin return mechanism. I don't know why people can't just throw their trash away. There's a garbage can literally two feet from the machine. But people are lazy, you know? Just like the guys who leave their empty drink cups on the slot machines. 

But yeah... I just feel really down most of the time. Like I'm just going through the motions.

Well, let's see... it's been about three weeks now. Yeah, right around the time the casino decided to reorganize our whole security operation and mess with my assignments. But anyway, they moved me from there to the penny slots section. It's just row after row of people sitting there like zombies, hitting the same button over and over. And the noise... it's this constant dinging and flashing lights. It gives you a headache after about an hour, but I have to sit there and watch it for eight. 

But yeah, it's been about three weeks since all that started, and that's when this whole heavy feeling really kicked in.

Yeah, pretty much every day. From the time I wake up until I go to sleep, it's just this constant drag. Even on my days off, I can't shake it. 

I actually tried to go to the hardware store on my last day off to get some new weather stripping for the back door. The draft in the kitchen has been driving my wife crazy. But I got to the aisle with all the tape and foam strips, and I just stood there staring at it. They have like fifty different kinds now. Some of them are made of this weird silicone stuff that's supposed to last ten years, but it costs like thirty bucks a roll. Thirty bucks! For some rubber tape! I remember when you could get a whole roll of the foam stuff for like three dollars. My dad used to use it on the old Chevy we had when I was a kid. The windows wouldn't roll up all the way, so he just jammed weather stripping in the gap. It looked terrible, but it kept the rain out, mostly. Except for that one time we drove through a huge storm in Nebraska... 

But yeah, anyway, I just ended up leaving the store without buying anything. I just didn't have the energy to figure it out. So yeah, it's been almost every day, all day.

Like I'm just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the heaviness and loss of interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... it's been about three weeks now. Yeah, right around the time the casino decided to reorganize our whole security operation and mess with my assignments. They brought in this new director, some guy from Vegas who thinks he knows everything about how a floor should run. He made everyone switch up their zones. \n\nI got moved from the high-roller tables—which is where the real action is, you know? You see some crazy stuff there. People dropping ten grand on one hand of baccarat like it's pocket change. I saw a guy once spill a whole martini on a dealer and just throw a hundred dollar chip at him to apologize. The dealer couldn't even say anything because the guy was a VIP. But anyway, they moved me from there to the penny slots section. It's just row after row of people sitting there like zombies, hitting the same button over and over. And the noise... it's this constant dinging and flashing lights. It gives you a headache after about an hour, but I have to sit there and watch it for eight. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about three weeks since all that started, and that's when this whole heavy feeling really kicked in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced days where you felt down or lost pleasure in activities almost every day for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much every day. From the time I wake up until I go to sleep, it's just this constant drag. Even on my days off, I can't shake it. \n\nI actually tried to go to the hardware store on my last day off to get some new weather stripping for the back door. The draft in the kitchen has been driving my wife crazy. But I got to the aisle with all the tape and foam strips, and I just stood there staring at it. They have like fifty different kinds now. Some of them are made of this weird silicone stuff that's supposed to last ten years, but it costs like thirty bucks a roll. Thirty bucks! For some rubber tape! I remember when you could get a whole roll of the foam stuff for like three dollars. My dad used to use it on the old Chevy we had when I was a kid. The windows wouldn't roll up all the way, so he just jammed weather stripping in the gap. It looked terrible, but it kept the rain out, mostly. Except for that one time we drove through a huge storm in Nebraska... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I just ended up leaving the store without buying anything. I just didn't have the energy to figure it out. So yeah, it's been almost every day, all day.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you noticed that you have less energy than usual or that even small tasks feel really tiring lately?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 39 / security_guard_or_gambling_surveillance_officer / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I guess so. I just don't have the energy for much of anything anymore. Even just taking out the trash feels like this huge chore. And we generate a lot of trash, especially since my wife started buying those meal prep kits. Have you seen those? They send you all the ingredients in these little plastic bags and containers. It's supposed to make cooking easier, but the amount of packaging is insane. You get one tiny sprig of thyme in a plastic clamshell that could hold a whole sandwich. We have to break down like three cardboard boxes a week just from those deliveries. 

The recycling guys get really annoyed if you don't break the boxes down flat. Last month, they actually left a note on our bin because I just shoved a box in there whole. It was written on this bright orange sticker. I didn't even know they carried those around. It makes you wonder how much time they spend writing notes instead of just picking up the recycling. My buddy Dave used to drive a garbage truck, and he said they had a quota for how many houses they had to hit an hour, so they never stopped to write notes. Dave's living in Florida now, though. Bought a boat. 

But yeah, I don't know... I just feel wiped out all the time. Everything just takes too much effort.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, actually. Some of the guys at work have pointed it out. Like, during our pre-shift briefings, they say I'm moving slower, and I'm definitely taking a lot longer to answer when someone talks to me. It's like my body and my mouth are just a step behind everybody else. 

Which is tough because our shift supervisor, Gary, he talks a mile a minute. He actually used to be an auctioneer down in Texas before he got into casino security. Can you imagine that? An auctioneer. He still does that fast-talking thing when he's going over the BOLO lists—the 'Be On the Lookout' sheets. He'll be rattling off descriptions of card counters and banned players so fast you can't even keep up. And the printer they use in the back office is this ancient laserjet that leaves these thick black streaks down the side of the page, so half the time you can't even see the suspect's face anyway. We've been putting in maintenance requests for a new printer for like six months, but corporate won't approve it. They say it's not in the budget right now. But they can afford to build a giant LED water fountain in the main lobby... it shoots water in time to Sinatra songs. 

But yeah, anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right. Yeah, my coworkers definitely noticed I'm just lagging behind everyone else.

History]\nDoctor: Have you experienced days where you felt down or lost pleasure in activities almost every day for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much every day. Some of them are made of this weird silicone stuff that's supposed to last ten years, but it costs like thirty bucks a roll. Thirty bucks! For some rubber tape! It's like my body and my mouth are just a step behind everybody else. \n\nWhich is tough because our shift supervisor, Gary, he talks a mile a minute. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, I wouldn't really say that. I don't feel worthless or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just show up and do my job. 

Speaking of the job, they actually just changed our uniform shirts. We used to have these nice cotton polos that breathed really well, especially in the summer when the AC on the casino floor gets overwhelmed by all the bodies. But now they switched us to this weird polyester blend. They say it's more "stain-resistant" or whatever, but it just traps all the heat. And the color is slightly different too. It's supposed to be navy blue, but it has this weird purple tint to it under the fluorescent lights. My wife said I look like a giant bruise when I put it on. She actually tried washing it with some vinegar to see if it would take the shine out of the fabric. I don't know where she gets these ideas. She watches a lot of those home hack videos on TikTok. Last week she tried to clean the grout in the bathroom with baking soda and a toothbrush. She was in there for three hours. The grout looks exactly the same, but now the bathroom smells like a science experiment. 

But no, to answer your question, I don't feel like I'm letting anyone down or anything. I just feel tired.

Except for that one time we drove through a huge storm in Nebraska... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I just ended up leaving the store without buying anything. I just didn't have the energy to figure it out. I just don't have the energy for much of anything anymore. Even just taking out the trash feels like this huge chore. And we generate a lot of trash, especially since my wife started buying those meal prep kits. Have you seen those? They send you all the ingredients in these little plastic bags and containers. It's supposed to make cooking easier, but the amount of packaging is insane. You get one tiny sprig of thyme in a plastic clamshell that could hold a whole sandwich. We have to break down like three cardboard boxes a week just from those deliveries. \n\nThe recycling guys get really annoyed if you don't break the boxes down flat. Sleep? No, not really. I mean, I sleep about the same as I always do. Usually out by like 11, up around 6:30. 

Though, getting out of bed has been harder lately because it's been getting so cold in the mornings. We live in this older house, built in like the '70s, and the insulation is just terrible. The windows are those single-pane ones with the aluminum frames, so they just bleed heat right out. You can actually feel a draft if you stand next to the sliding glass door in the kitchen. My neighbor, Frank, he just had all his windows replaced with those double-pane argon gas ones. He was telling me about it over the fence on Sunday. He said his heating bill went down by like forty bucks a month. But the installation took three days, and they left a bunch of fiberglass dust all over his living room carpet. He had to rent one of those Rug Doctor machines from the grocery store to get it out. You know the ones? They're always sitting right by the automatic doors, looking like some kind of weird red robot. I always wonder how often they actually clean those machines. Like, you're renting something to clean your house, but who cleans the cleaner? 

But anyway, yeah, my sleep is fine. I haven't noticed any changes with that.

Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, actually, that's kind of why I came in. For the last few weeks, I've barely had any appetite at all. Even when I get home after a shift and try to eat something I usually like, it just sits there on the plate while I force down a few bites. 

Like last night, my wife made her famous meatloaf. She uses this special glaze on top—it's like ketchup, brown sugar, and a little bit of mustard. Her mom gave her the recipe. Her mom is from Ohio, and apparently, that's how they do it out there. They also put chili on top of spaghetti, which I just don't understand. I mean, chili goes in a bowl with some cheese and maybe a few Fritos, right? But out in Cincinnati, they pile it on top of noodles. My brother-in-law went to a conference out there once for his plumbing business. He said they call it a "three-way" when it's chili, spaghetti, and cheese. He brought back this tin of the spice mix they use, and it has cinnamon in it. Cinnamon! In chili! Can you believe that? It smells like a Yankee Candle when you cook it. We made it once for the Super Bowl two years ago, and half the guys wouldn't even touch it. Gary—my supervisor, the fast talker—he actually brought his own Tupperware of wings because he said he doesn't trust "Midwestern culinary experiments." 

But yeah, anyway, the meatloaf last night... I usually love it, but I just couldn't even eat half a slice. I just haven't been hungry.

Some of them are made of this weird silicone stuff that's supposed to last ten years, but it costs like thirty bucks a roll. Thirty bucks! For some rubber tape! I remember when you could get a whole roll of the foam stuff for like three dollars. My dad used to use it on the old Chevy we had when I was a kid. The windows wouldn't roll up all the way, so he just jammed weather stripping in the gap. It looked terrible, but it kept the rain out, mostly. Except for that one time we drove through a huge storm in Nebraska... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I just ended up leaving the store without buying anything. I just didn't have the energy to figure it out. So yeah, it's been almost every day, all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have less energy than usual or that even small tasks feel really tiring lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I just don't have the energy for much of anything anymore. Even just taking out the trash feels like this huge chore. And we generate a lot of trash, especially since my wife started buying those meal prep kits. Have you seen those? They send you all the ingredients in these little plastic bags and containers. It's supposed to make cooking easier, but the amount of packaging is insane. You get one tiny sprig of thyme in a plastic clamshell that could hold a whole sandwich. We have to break down like three cardboard boxes a week just from those deliveries. \n\nThe recycling guys get really annoyed if you don't break the boxes down flat. Last month, they actually left a note on our bin because I just shoved a box in there whole. It was written on this bright orange sticker. I didn't even know they carried those around. It makes you wonder how much time they spend writing notes instead of just picking up the recycling. My buddy Dave used to drive a garbage truck, and he said they had a quota for how many houses they had to hit an hour, so they never stopped to write notes. Dave's living in Florida now, though. Bought a boat. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know... I just feel wiped out all the time. Everything just takes too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, to the point that others might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. He'll be rattling off descriptions of card counters and banned players so fast you can't even keep up. Last time I was in there to pick up my pants, he was literally yelling at the little TV on his counter, waving a steamer wand around because some character named Victor was doing something shady. It’s funny because Mr. Kim barely speaks to me when I drop my stuff off, but he is fully invested in all the drama in Genoa City. I always wonder how he gets any actual dry cleaning done when he's glued to the screen like that. \n\nBut yeah, my belt is definitely down a notch since, like I said, I’ve just been forcing down a few bites of dinner here and there.\n\n[Last Question]\nWould you say that your mood has been persistently low or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day, during these last few weeks?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 39 / security_guard_or_gambling_surveillance_officer / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressed_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Most days I feel like I’m carrying this heavy, flat feeling around, like even when I’m home with my family or sitting in the break room, I’m just not really there.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I guess so. Most days I feel like I'm carrying this heavy, flat feeling around. Like, even when I'm home with my family or sitting in the break room at work, I'm just not really there, you know? 

The break room is actually where I spend most of my time when I'm not on the floor. It's this tiny little room tucked behind the surveillance monitors. They have this old vending machine in there that still takes the old dollar bills, the ones without the big numbers on the back. It's always getting jammed. Last week, Terry—he's the new guy on the swing shift—tried to get a bag of Funyuns, and it got stuck halfway down. He spent like twenty minutes rocking that heavy machine back and forth. I thought he was going to tip the whole thing over and crush himself. Which would be a nightmare for the paperwork, let me tell you. Casino management hates paperwork. They make us fill out these incident reports in triplicate for every little thing. Like if someone spills a drink near a slot machine, boom, triplicate report. I don't even know where all those copies go. Probably some giant warehouse somewhere, like at the end of Raiders of the Lost Ark. You ever see that movie? Where they box up the Ark of the Covenant and just leave it in this massive room full of identical boxes? That's what I imagine our HR department looks like. Just aisles and aisles of spilled drink reports. 

But, yeah, back to what you were saying. I just feel heavy and flat most of the time lately.

People dropping ten grand on one hand of baccarat like it's pocket change. I saw a guy once spill a whole martini on a dealer and just throw a hundred dollar chip at him to apologize. It's just row after row of people sitting there like zombies, hitting the same button over and over. And the noise... it's this constant dinging and flashing lights. It gives you a headache after about an hour, but I have to sit there and watch it for eight. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about three weeks since all that started, and that's when this whole heavy feeling really kicked in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced days where you felt down or lost pleasure in activities almost every day for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much every day. Some of them are made of this weird silicone stuff that's supposed to last ten years, but it costs like thirty bucks a roll. Thirty bucks! For some rubber tape! I remember when you could get a whole roll of the foam stuff for like three dollars. My dad used to use it on the old Chevy we had when I was a kid. The windows wouldn't roll up all the way, so he just jammed weather stripping in the gap. It looked terrible, but it kept the rain out, mostly. Except for that one time we drove through a huge storm in Nebraska... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I just ended up leaving the store without buying anything. I just didn't have the energy to figure it out. So yeah, it's been almost every day, all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have less energy than usual or that even small tasks feel really tiring lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I just don't have the energy for much of anything anymore. Even just taking out the trash feels like this huge chore. And we generate a lot of trash, especially since my wife started buying those meal prep kits. Have you seen those? They send you all the ingredients in these little plastic bags and containers. It's supposed to make cooking easier, but the amount of packaging is insane. You get one tiny sprig of thyme in a plastic clamshell that could hold a whole sandwich. We have to break down like three cardboard boxes a week just from those deliveries. \n\nThe recycling guys get really annoyed if you don't break the boxes down flat. Last month, they actually left a note on our bin because I just shoved a box in there whole. It was written on this bright orange sticker. I didn't even know they carried those around. It makes you wonder how much time they spend writing notes instead of just picking up the recycling. My buddy Dave used to drive a garbage truck, and he said they had a quota for how many houses they had to hit an hour, so they never stopped to write notes. Dave's living in Florida now, though. Bought a boat. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know... I just feel wiped out all the time. Everything just takes too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, to the point that others might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. Some of the guys at work have pointed it out. Like, during our pre-shift briefings, they say I'm moving slower, and I'm definitely taking a lot longer to answer when someone talks to me. It's like my body and my mouth are just a step behind everybody else. \n\nWhich is tough because our shift supervisor, Gary, he talks a mile a minute. He actually used to be an auctioneer down in Texas before he got into casino security. Can you imagine that? An auctioneer. He still does that fast-talking thing when he's going over the BOLO lists—the 'Be On the Lookout' sheets. He'll be rattling off descriptions of card counters and banned players so fast you can't even keep up. And the printer they use in the back office is this ancient laserjet that leaves these thick black streaks down the side of the page, so half the time you can't even see the suspect's face anyway. We've been putting in maintenance requests for a new printer for like six months, but corporate won't approve it. They say it's not in the budget right now. But they can afford to build a giant LED water fountain in the main lobby... it shoots water in time to Sinatra songs. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right. Yeah, my coworkers definitely noticed I'm just lagging behind everyone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not worth much or like you've let people down lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't really say that. I don't feel worthless or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just show up and do my job. \n\nSpeaking of the job, they actually just changed our uniform shirts. We used to have these nice cotton polos that breathed really well, especially in the summer when the AC on the casino floor gets overwhelmed by all the bodies. But now they switched us to this weird polyester blend. They say it's more \"stain-resistant\" or whatever, but it just traps all the heat. And the color is slightly different too. It's supposed to be navy blue, but it has this weird purple tint to it under the fluorescent lights. My wife said I look like a giant bruise when I put it on. She actually tried washing it with some vinegar to see if it would take the shine out of the fabric. I don't know where she gets these ideas. She watches a lot of those home hack videos on TikTok. Last week she tried to clean the grout in the bathroom with baking soda and a toothbrush. She was in there for three hours. The grout looks exactly the same, but now the bathroom smells like a science experiment. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I don't feel like I'm letting anyone down or anything. I just feel tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, not really. I mean, I sleep about the same as I always do. Usually out by like 11, up around 6:30. \n\nThough, getting out of bed has been harder lately because it's been getting so cold in the mornings. We live in this older house, built in like the '70s, and the insulation is just terrible. The windows are those single-pane ones with the aluminum frames, so they just bleed heat right out. You can actually feel a draft if you stand next to the sliding glass door in the kitchen. My neighbor, Frank, he just had all his windows replaced with those double-pane argon gas ones. He was telling me about it over the fence on Sunday. He said his heating bill went down by like forty bucks a month. But the installation took three days, and they left a bunch of fiberglass dust all over his living room carpet. He had to rent one of those Rug Doctor machines from the grocery store to get it out. You know the ones? They're always sitting right by the automatic doors, looking like some kind of weird red robot. I always wonder how often they actually clean those machines. Like, you're renting something to clean your house, but who cleans the cleaner? \n\nBut anyway, yeah, my sleep is fine. I haven't noticed any changes with that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently without trying, or noticed a significant decrease in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's kind of why I came in. Even when I get home after a shift and try to eat something I usually like, it just sits there on the plate while I force down a few bites. \n\nLike last night, my wife made her famous meatloaf. She uses this special glaze on top—it's like ketchup, brown sugar, and a little bit of mustard. Her mom gave her the recipe. Her mom is from Ohio, and apparently, that's how they do it out there. They also put chili on top of spaghetti, which I just don't understand. I mean, chili goes in a bowl with some cheese and maybe a few Fritos, right? But out in Cincinnati, they pile it on top of noodles. My brother-in-law went to a conference out there once for his plumbing business. He said they call it a \"three-way\" when it's chili, spaghetti, and cheese. He brought back this tin of the spice mix they use, and it has cinnamon in it. Cinnamon! In chili! Can you believe that? It smells like a Yankee Candle when you cook it. We made it once for the Super Bowl two years ago, and half the guys wouldn't even touch it. Gary—my supervisor, the fast talker—he actually brought his own Tupperware of wings because he said he doesn't trust \"Midwestern culinary experiments.\" \n\nBut yeah, anyway, the meatloaf last night... I usually love it, but I just couldn't even eat half a slice. I just haven't been hungry.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently without trying to?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was just saying about the meatloaf, I've probably dropped a few pounds just because my appetite has been so nonexistent these last few weeks. I just can't bring myself to eat much. I actually noticed it the other day when I was putting on my uniform pants—they're definitely fitting a lot looser around the waist. \n\nWe have to wear these specific black slacks for work, and the casino makes us take them to this one specific dry cleaner over on Elm Street to keep them looking sharp. The guy who runs it, Mr. Kim, he's always in there watching those daytime soap operas. I didn't even know they still made those, you know? Like The Young and the Restless or whatever. He gets so worked up over them. Last time I was in there to pick up my pants, he was literally yelling at the little TV on his counter, waving a steamer wand around because some character named Victor was doing something shady. No, I mean, I'm not planning to hurt myself or anything like that. But... I don't know, lately I do keep having these thoughts that maybe it wouldn't be the worst thing if I just didn't wake up. Like, if I didn't have to keep pushing through all this heavy stuff every single day, it would just be easier. 

Waking up is the hardest part anyway. I have my phone alarm set to that horrible radar sound. You know the Apple one? It’s just so loud and jarring. It spikes your blood pressure the second it goes off in the dark. My buddy Dave—he works the graveyard shift with me sometimes—he actually uses a rooster crowing for his alarm. Which is completely insane because he lives in this massive apartment complex over by the interstate. His neighbors absolutely hate him for it. They actually complained to the HOA about him. Can you believe an apartment building has an HOA? They are ruthless over there, too. They literally measure the height of the welcome mats outside your door to make sure they're standard issue. Dave got a fifty-dollar fine last month because his mat had a picture of a golden retriever on it instead of being plain black. He doesn't even own a dog! He just bought the mat on clearance at Target because it was three bucks. Target's clearance section used to be so good, but now it's mostly just broken plastic bins and weird-smelling candles. 

But yeah, I wouldn't actually do anything to hurt myself. I just... wouldn't mind if the alarm didn't go off, I guess.

Just last week we had this guy try to sneak a bunch of chips into his jacket at the blackjack table, but we had him on camera three the whole time. I mean, twelve bucks for a combo meal now? It's getting ridiculous.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you usually enjoy, like food or other activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's not just the food. I used to really look forward to my morning runs, playing my guitar, cooking dinner... but lately, I keep skipping all of it. They just feel like chores that I can't get myself to care about anymore. \n\nWhich is a shame, because I have this really nice Fender Stratocaster. Bought it off a guy who lost big at the craps tables a few years back. He just walked right out to the parking lot and sold it to me from his trunk for like two hundred bucks. Honestly, I think it was his kid's or something, it had these little alien stickers on the back of the neck. I spent a whole weekend peeling them off with some Goo Gone. That stuff smells terrible, kind of like oranges mixed with gasoline, but it works on everything. We use it at the casino sometimes when people stick gum under the card tables. People are so gross, you wouldn't believe it. \n\nBut yeah... I just haven't even taken the guitar out of the case in weeks. I just don't care to.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed most of the time lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess you could say that. Even when I'm home with the wife and kids, or just sitting in the break room at work, I feel like I'm just... not really there. \n\nSpeaking of the break room, they finally fixed the vending machine. For like three months, it would just eat your quarters if you tried to buy the peanut butter crackers. I must have lost at least five bucks to that thing. The maintenance guy, Frank—he's this older guy, always wears these suspenders with tools hanging off them—he finally got in there and fixed it. Said a candy bar wrapper got jammed in the coin return mechanism. I don't know why people can't just throw their trash away. There's a garbage can literally two feet from the machine. But people are lazy, you know? Just like the guys who leave their empty drink cups on the slot machines. \n\nBut yeah... I just feel really down most of the time. Like I'm just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with the heaviness and loss of interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... it's been about three weeks now. Yeah, right around the time the casino decided to reorganize our whole security operation and mess with my assignments. They brought in this new director, some guy from Vegas who thinks he knows everything about how a floor should run. He made everyone switch up their zones. \n\nI got moved from the high-roller tables—which is where the real action is, you know? You see some crazy stuff there. People dropping ten grand on one hand of baccarat like it's pocket change. I saw a guy once spill a whole martini on a dealer and just throw a hundred dollar chip at him to apologize. The dealer couldn't even say anything because the guy was a VIP. But anyway, they moved me from there to the penny slots section. It's just row after row of people sitting there like zombies, hitting the same button over and over. And the noise... it's this constant dinging and flashing lights. It gives you a headache after about an hour, but I have to sit there and watch it for eight. \n\nBut yeah, it's been about three weeks since all that started, and that's when this whole heavy feeling really kicked in.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced days where you felt down or lost pleasure in activities almost every day for most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much every day. From the time I wake up until I go to sleep, it's just this constant drag. Even on my days off, I can't shake it. \n\nI actually tried to go to the hardware store on my last day off to get some new weather stripping for the back door. The draft in the kitchen has been driving my wife crazy. But I got to the aisle with all the tape and foam strips, and I just stood there staring at it. They have like fifty different kinds now. Some of them are made of this weird silicone stuff that's supposed to last ten years, but it costs like thirty bucks a roll. Thirty bucks! For some rubber tape! I remember when you could get a whole roll of the foam stuff for like three dollars. My dad used to use it on the old Chevy we had when I was a kid. The windows wouldn't roll up all the way, so he just jammed weather stripping in the gap. It looked terrible, but it kept the rain out, mostly. Except for that one time we drove through a huge storm in Nebraska... \n\nBut yeah, anyway, I just ended up leaving the store without buying anything. I just didn't have the energy to figure it out. So yeah, it's been almost every day, all day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you have less energy than usual or that even small tasks feel really tiring lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. I just don't have the energy for much of anything anymore. Even just taking out the trash feels like this huge chore. And we generate a lot of trash, especially since my wife started buying those meal prep kits. Have you seen those? They send you all the ingredients in these little plastic bags and containers. It's supposed to make cooking easier, but the amount of packaging is insane. You get one tiny sprig of thyme in a plastic clamshell that could hold a whole sandwich. We have to break down like three cardboard boxes a week just from those deliveries. \n\nThe recycling guys get really annoyed if you don't break the boxes down flat. Last month, they actually left a note on our bin because I just shoved a box in there whole. It was written on this bright orange sticker. I didn't even know they carried those around. It makes you wonder how much time they spend writing notes instead of just picking up the recycling. My buddy Dave used to drive a garbage truck, and he said they had a quota for how many houses they had to hit an hour, so they never stopped to write notes. Dave's living in Florida now, though. Bought a boat. \n\nBut yeah, I don't know... I just feel wiped out all the time. Everything just takes too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the contrary, feeling restless or fidgety, to the point that others might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually. Some of the guys at work have pointed it out. Like, during our pre-shift briefings, they say I'm moving slower, and I'm definitely taking a lot longer to answer when someone talks to me. It's like my body and my mouth are just a step behind everybody else. \n\nWhich is tough because our shift supervisor, Gary, he talks a mile a minute. He actually used to be an auctioneer down in Texas before he got into casino security. Can you imagine that? An auctioneer. He still does that fast-talking thing when he's going over the BOLO lists—the 'Be On the Lookout' sheets. He'll be rattling off descriptions of card counters and banned players so fast you can't even keep up. And the printer they use in the back office is this ancient laserjet that leaves these thick black streaks down the side of the page, so half the time you can't even see the suspect's face anyway. We've been putting in maintenance requests for a new printer for like six months, but corporate won't approve it. They say it's not in the budget right now. But they can afford to build a giant LED water fountain in the main lobby... it shoots water in time to Sinatra songs. \n\nBut yeah, anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right. Yeah, my coworkers definitely noticed I'm just lagging behind everyone else.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're not worth much or like you've let people down lately?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't really say that. I don't feel worthless or anything like that. I just... I don't know, I just show up and do my job. \n\nSpeaking of the job, they actually just changed our uniform shirts. We used to have these nice cotton polos that breathed really well, especially in the summer when the AC on the casino floor gets overwhelmed by all the bodies. But now they switched us to this weird polyester blend. They say it's more \"stain-resistant\" or whatever, but it just traps all the heat. And the color is slightly different too. It's supposed to be navy blue, but it has this weird purple tint to it under the fluorescent lights. My wife said I look like a giant bruise when I put it on. She actually tried washing it with some vinegar to see if it would take the shine out of the fabric. I don't know where she gets these ideas. She watches a lot of those home hack videos on TikTok. Last week she tried to clean the grout in the bathroom with baking soda and a toothbrush. She was in there for three hours. The grout looks exactly the same, but now the bathroom smells like a science experiment. \n\nBut no, to answer your question, I don't feel like I'm letting anyone down or anything. I just feel tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like having trouble falling asleep, waking up during the night, or sleeping much more than usual?\n\nPatient: Sleep? No, not really. I mean, I sleep about the same as I always do. Usually out by like 11, up around 6:30. \n\nThough, getting out of bed has been harder lately because it's been getting so cold in the mornings. We live in this older house, built in like the '70s, and the insulation is just terrible. The windows are those single-pane ones with the aluminum frames, so they just bleed heat right out. You can actually feel a draft if you stand next to the sliding glass door in the kitchen. My neighbor, Frank, he just had all his windows replaced with those double-pane argon gas ones. He was telling me about it over the fence on Sunday. He said his heating bill went down by like forty bucks a month. But the installation took three days, and they left a bunch of fiberglass dust all over his living room carpet. He had to rent one of those Rug Doctor machines from the grocery store to get it out. You know the ones? They're always sitting right by the automatic doors, looking like some kind of weird red robot. I always wonder how often they actually clean those machines. Like, you're renting something to clean your house, but who cleans the cleaner? \n\nBut anyway, yeah, my sleep is fine. I haven't noticed any changes with that.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently without trying, or noticed a significant decrease in your appetite?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's kind of why I came in. For the last few weeks, I've barely had any appetite at all. Even when I get home after a shift and try to eat something I usually like, it just sits there on the plate while I force down a few bites. \n\nLike last night, my wife made her famous meatloaf. She uses this special glaze on top—it's like ketchup, brown sugar, and a little bit of mustard. Her mom gave her the recipe. Her mom is from Ohio, and apparently, that's how they do it out there. They also put chili on top of spaghetti, which I just don't understand. I mean, chili goes in a bowl with some cheese and maybe a few Fritos, right? But out in Cincinnati, they pile it on top of noodles. My brother-in-law went to a conference out there once for his plumbing business. He said they call it a \"three-way\" when it's chili, spaghetti, and cheese. He brought back this tin of the spice mix they use, and it has cinnamon in it. Cinnamon! In chili! Can you believe that? It smells like a Yankee Candle when you cook it. We made it once for the Super Bowl two years ago, and half the guys wouldn't even touch it. Gary—my supervisor, the fast talker—he actually brought his own Tupperware of wings because he said he doesn't trust \"Midwestern culinary experiments.\" \n\nBut yeah, anyway, the meatloaf last night... I usually love it, but I just couldn't even eat half a slice. I just haven't been hungry.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently without trying to?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, like I was just saying about the meatloaf, I've probably dropped a few pounds just because my appetite has been so nonexistent these last few weeks. I just can't bring myself to eat much. I actually noticed it the other day when I was putting on my uniform pants—they're definitely fitting a lot looser around the waist. \n\nWe have to wear these specific black slacks for work, and the casino makes us take them to this one specific dry cleaner over on Elm Street to keep them looking sharp. The guy who runs it, Mr. Kim, he's always in there watching those daytime soap operas. I didn't even know they still made those, you know? Like The Young and the Restless or whatever. He gets so worked up over them. Last time I was in there to pick up my pants, he was literally yelling at the little TV on his counter, waving a steamer wand around because some character named Victor was doing something shady. It’s funny because Mr. Kim barely speaks to me when I drop my stuff off, but he is fully invested in all the drama in Genoa City. I always wonder how he gets any actual dry cleaning done when he's glued to the screen like that. \n\nBut yeah, my belt is definitely down a notch since, like I said, I’ve just been forcing down a few bites of dinner here and there.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been persistently low or depressed for most of the day, nearly every day, during these last few weeks?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Most days I feel like I'm carrying this heavy, flat feeling around. Like, even when I'm home with my family or sitting in the break room at work, I'm just not really there, you know? \n\nThe break room is actually where I spend most of my time when I'm not on the floor. It's this tiny little room tucked behind the surveillance monitors. They have this old vending machine in there that still takes the old dollar bills, the ones without the big numbers on the back. It's always getting jammed. Last week, Terry—he's the new guy on the swing shift—tried to get a bag of Funyuns, and it got stuck halfway down. He spent like twenty minutes rocking that heavy machine back and forth. I thought he was going to tip the whole thing over and crush himself. Which would be a nightmare for the paperwork, let me tell you. Casino management hates paperwork. They make us fill out these incident reports in triplicate for every little thing. Like if someone spills a drink near a slot machine, boom, triplicate report. I don't even know where all those copies go. Probably some giant warehouse somewhere, like at the end of Raiders of the Lost Ark. You ever see that movie? Where they box up the Ark of the Covenant and just leave it in this massive room full of identical boxes? That's what I imagine our HR department looks like. Just aisles and aisles of spilled drink reports. \n\nBut, yeah, back to what you were saying. I just feel heavy and flat most of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: No, I mean, I'm not planning to hurt myself or anything like that. But... I don't know, lately I do keep having these thoughts that maybe it wouldn't be the worst thing if I just didn't wake up. Like, if I didn't have to keep pushing through all this heavy stuff every single day, it would just be easier. \n\nWaking up is the hardest part anyway. I have my phone alarm set to that horrible radar sound. You know the Apple one? It’s just so loud and jarring. It spikes your blood pressure the second it goes off in the dark. My buddy Dave—he works the graveyard shift with me sometimes—he actually uses a rooster crowing for his alarm. Which is completely insane because he lives in this massive apartment complex over by the interstate. His neighbors absolutely hate him for it. They actually complained to the HOA about him. Can you believe an apartment building has an HOA? They are ruthless over there, too. They literally measure the height of the welcome mats outside your door to make sure they're standard issue. Dave got a fifty-dollar fine last month because his mat had a picture of a golden retriever on it instead of being plain black. He doesn't even own a dog! He just bought the mat on clearance at Target because it was three bucks. Target's clearance section used to be so good, but now it's mostly just broken plastic bins and weird-smelling candles. \n\nBut yeah, I wouldn't actually do anything to hurt myself. I just... wouldn't mind if the alarm didn't go off, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, it's definitely been at least two weeks. Honestly, it's been going on for about three weeks now. It all started right around the time the casino reorganized our whole security operation and switched up my assignments. 

They brought in this new consulting firm from Vegas to "optimize" our floor coverage, which basically just meant moving everyone around and messing up our schedules. The guy leading the consulting team, his name was Chad, and he wore these ridiculously pointy dress shoes. I don't even know how you walk in those without tripping over your own feet. He kept pacing around the surveillance room, talking about "synergy" and "dynamic threat assessment." My buddy Dave—the one with the rooster alarm—he asked Chad if "dynamic threat assessment" included watching out for people sneaking extra crab legs from the buffet. Because, let me tell you, the buffet on Friday nights is a warzone. People will straight up tackle each other when a fresh tray of snow crab comes out. We actually had to permanently station a guard by the carving station because this one lady tried to take an entire prime rib roast in her purse. A whole roast! She lined her giant leather tote bag with tin foil and just went for it. The chef was chasing her through the slot machines with a pair of tongs. It was wild. 

But yeah, ever since they changed my assignments about three weeks ago, I've just had that flat, down feeling pretty much all day, every day.

Most days I feel like I'm carrying this heavy, flat feeling around. It's always getting jammed. Last week, Terry—he's the new guy on the swing shift—tried to get a bag of Funyuns, and it got stuck halfway down. He spent like twenty minutes rocking that heavy machine back and forth. I thought he was going to tip the whole thing over and crush himself. Which would be a nightmare for the paperwork, let me tell you. Casino management hates paperwork. They make us fill out these incident reports in triplicate for every little thing. Like if someone spills a drink near a slot machine, boom, triplicate report. I don't even know where all those copies go. Probably some giant warehouse somewhere, like at the end of Raiders of the Lost Ark. You ever see that movie? Where they box up the Ark of the Covenant and just leave it in this massive room full of identical boxes? That's what I imagine our HR department looks like. Just aisles and aisles of spilled drink reports. \n\nBut, yeah, back to what you were saying. I just feel heavy and flat most of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: No, I mean, I'm not planning to hurt myself or anything like that. My buddy Dave—he works the graveyard shift with me sometimes—he actually uses a rooster crowing for his alarm. Which is completely insane because he lives in this massive apartment complex over by the interstate. His neighbors absolutely hate him for it. They actually complained to the HOA about him. Can you believe an apartment building has an HOA? They are ruthless over there, too. They literally measure the height of the welcome mats outside your door to make sure they're standard issue. Dave got a fifty-dollar fine last month because his mat had a picture of a golden retriever on it instead of being plain black. He doesn't even own a dog! He just bought the mat on clearance at Target because it was three bucks. Target's clearance section used to be so good, but now it's mostly just broken plastic bins and weird-smelling candles. \n\nBut yeah, I wouldn't actually do anything to hurt myself. I just... wouldn't mind if the alarm didn't go off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that for at least two weeks in a row, you’ve felt down or flat most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely been at least two weeks. We actually had to permanently station a guard by the carving station because this one lady tried to take an entire prime rib roast in her purse. A whole roast! She lined her giant leather tote bag with tin foil and just went for it. The chef was chasing her through the slot machines with a pair of tongs. Yeah, actually, I have. It's like my brain is just stuck in the mud. When I’m watching the CCTV feeds or trying to write up those incident reports I was talking about, my mind just completely drifts off. I have to reread the same lines over and over again just to figure out what I'm looking at.

They're always out there goofing off instead of parking cars. Especially this one kid, Ricky. I had to go down there and break it up so nobody threw a punch. I mean... I'm not really sure. I don't know if I'd say it's noticeably worse than other times I've felt low. I've been bummed out before, obviously. He kept pacing around the surveillance room, talking about \"synergy\" and \"dynamic threat assessment.\" My buddy Dave—the one with the rooster alarm—he asked Chad if \"dynamic threat assessment\" included watching out for people sneaking extra crab legs from the buffet. It's like my brain is just stuck in the mud. When I’m watching the CCTV feeds or trying to write up those incident reports I was talking about, my mind just completely drifts off. I have to reread the same lines over and over again just to figure out what I'm looking at. \n\nSpeaking of the CCTV feeds, they actually just upgraded all our monitors in the surveillance room to these massive 4K screens. It's insane how much detail you can see now. It's almost a little creepy, honestly. Like, I can zoom in and see the exact brand of cigarettes the valet guys are smoking out by the front entrance. They're always out there goofing off instead of parking cars. Especially this one kid, Ricky. He drives this absolute junker of a Honda Civic, but he walks around acting like he personally owns all the Porsches and Maseratis he parks. I don't really think so. Not for two whole weeks, anyway. I mean, I guess I lost interest in my bowling league a few years back, but that wasn't like a... a mood thing. That was just because it got way too intense.

I just... wouldn't mind if the alarm didn't go off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that for at least two weeks in a row, you’ve felt down or flat most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely been at least two weeks. Honestly, it's been going on for about three weeks now. It all started right around the time the casino reorganized our whole security operation and switched up my assignments. \n\nThey brought in this new consulting firm from Vegas to \"optimize\" our floor coverage, which basically just meant moving everyone around and messing up our schedules. The guy leading the consulting team, his name was Chad, and he wore these ridiculously pointy dress shoes. I don't even know how you walk in those without tripping over your own feet. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Wait, didn't you just ask me that? But yeah, like I was saying, no. I haven't really had a time where I felt this flat for two weeks straight before. 

Two weeks is a weird amount of time to track, though, especially in my line of work. Working in a casino, time just sort of... evaporates. There are zero windows in the surveillance room. None on the main gaming floor either, obviously. It's a psychological trick, you know? Keep the gamblers from seeing the sun come up so they lose track of time and keep spending. They pump in oxygen, too. At least, that's what my buddy Marcus swears they do. Marcus works the high-limit craps tables. He's got this crazy conspiracy theory that the pit bosses are secretly venting pure oxygen through the AC grates to keep the whales awake. 

He actually bought this little handheld air quality monitor off the internet last year just to prove it. He was sneaking it onto the floor in his vest pocket, acting like some kind of undercover spy. The problem is, he forgot to turn off the warning alarm on the device. So right in the middle of this massive run—some guy was rolling for a twenty-thousand-dollar pot—Marcus's pocket starts shrieking like a smoke detector. My guys on the floor had to basically tackle him because they thought someone had rigged a device or something. It was an absolute circus. He almost got fired, but the casino manager just thought he was an idiot and suspended him for a week instead. 

But anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, right, feeling down in the past. Yeah, no, never for two whole weeks like this. Just this time.

Which would be a nightmare for the paperwork, let me tell you. Casino management hates paperwork. They make us fill out these incident reports in triplicate for every little thing. Like if someone spills a drink near a slot machine, boom, triplicate report. I don't even know where all those copies go. Probably some giant warehouse somewhere, like at the end of Raiders of the Lost Ark. You ever see that movie? Where they box up the Ark of the Covenant and just leave it in this massive room full of identical boxes? That's what I imagine our HR department looks like. Just aisles and aisles of spilled drink reports. \n\nBut, yeah, back to what you were saying. I just feel heavy and flat most of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about hurting yourself or that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: No, I mean, I'm not planning to hurt myself or anything like that. But... I don't know, lately I do keep having these thoughts that maybe it wouldn't be the worst thing if I just didn't wake up. Like, if I didn't have to keep pushing through all this heavy stuff every single day, it would just be easier. \n\nWaking up is the hardest part anyway. I have my phone alarm set to that horrible radar sound. You know the Apple one? It’s just so loud and jarring. It spikes your blood pressure the second it goes off in the dark. My buddy Dave—he works the graveyard shift with me sometimes—he actually uses a rooster crowing for his alarm. Which is completely insane because he lives in this massive apartment complex over by the interstate. His neighbors absolutely hate him for it. They actually complained to the HOA about him. Can you believe an apartment building has an HOA? They are ruthless over there, too. They literally measure the height of the welcome mats outside your door to make sure they're standard issue. Dave got a fifty-dollar fine last month because his mat had a picture of a golden retriever on it instead of being plain black. He doesn't even own a dog! He just bought the mat on clearance at Target because it was three bucks. Target's clearance section used to be so good, but now it's mostly just broken plastic bins and weird-smelling candles. \n\nBut yeah, I wouldn't actually do anything to hurt myself. I just... wouldn't mind if the alarm didn't go off, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that for at least two weeks in a row, you’ve felt down or flat most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely been at least two weeks. Honestly, it's been going on for about three weeks now. It all started right around the time the casino reorganized our whole security operation and switched up my assignments. \n\nThey brought in this new consulting firm from Vegas to \"optimize\" our floor coverage, which basically just meant moving everyone around and messing up our schedules. The guy leading the consulting team, his name was Chad, and he wore these ridiculously pointy dress shoes. I don't even know how you walk in those without tripping over your own feet. He kept pacing around the surveillance room, talking about \"synergy\" and \"dynamic threat assessment.\" My buddy Dave—the one with the rooster alarm—he asked Chad if \"dynamic threat assessment\" included watching out for people sneaking extra crab legs from the buffet. Because, let me tell you, the buffet on Friday nights is a warzone. People will straight up tackle each other when a fresh tray of snow crab comes out. We actually had to permanently station a guard by the carving station because this one lady tried to take an entire prime rib roast in her purse. A whole roast! She lined her giant leather tote bag with tin foil and just went for it. The chef was chasing her through the slot machines with a pair of tongs. It was wild. \n\nBut yeah, ever since they changed my assignments about three weeks ago, I've just had that flat, down feeling pretty much all day, every day.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that your thoughts have been moving more slowly than usual, like it's harder to process things or make decisions?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have. It's like my brain is just stuck in the mud. When I’m watching the CCTV feeds or trying to write up those incident reports I was talking about, my mind just completely drifts off. I have to reread the same lines over and over again just to figure out what I'm looking at. \n\nSpeaking of the CCTV feeds, they actually just upgraded all our monitors in the surveillance room to these massive 4K screens. It's insane how much detail you can see now. It's almost a little creepy, honestly. Like, I can zoom in and see the exact brand of cigarettes the valet guys are smoking out by the front entrance. They're always out there goofing off instead of parking cars. Especially this one kid, Ricky. He drives this absolute junker of a Honda Civic, but he walks around acting like he personally owns all the Porsches and Maseratis he parks. He actually scratched a guy's Tesla a few weeks ago because he was trying to park it one-handed while eating a powdered donut. There was white powder all over the steering wheel, all over the custom black leather seats... the owner was losing his mind, just screaming right there in the driveway. I had to go down there and break it up so nobody threw a punch. Which, of course, meant writing up another one of those massive triplicate reports for HR. \n\nBut yeah, when I was trying to type that one up, it took me forever. I just couldn't hold onto a thought, and I ended up making all these dumb mistakes on the form that I normally wouldn’t make. It just takes me so much longer to process anything right now.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that this episode of feeling down and losing interest in things is noticeably worse than any past periods when you might have felt low?\n\nPatient: I mean... I'm not really sure. I don't know if I'd say it's noticeably worse than other times I've felt low. I've been bummed out before, obviously. Like last year when they made us work double shifts over the holidays because half the staff got the flu. That was miserable. And we had to patrol the parking garage on foot the whole time because our security golf cart broke down. \n\nWe have this little electric cart we use for the structure, and the battery just completely died right on level four. Level four is the absolute worst because that's the VIP overflow parking, and the overhead lights are always flickering up there. Maintenance keeps saying it's a wiring issue, but maintenance is basically just this one guy, Gary, who spends half his shift sleeping down in the boiler room. I actually caught him on the cameras a few months ago making a literal hammock out of industrial shrink wrap. Like, the heavy-duty plastic wrap they use for shipping pallets of toilet paper and playing cards? He had it strung up between two giant water pipes. Honestly, you almost have to respect the ingenuity. He looked pretty comfortable, too, until the shift manager caught him. Gary tried to claim he was testing the tensile strength of the pipes, which is just the funniest excuse I've ever heard. \n\nBut yeah, walking that garage in the freezing cold for two weeks straight was pretty depressing. So, I don't know. I wouldn't say how I feel right now is way worse than anything else in the past. I haven't really noticed it being more severe or anything, I guess. It's just... heavy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting two weeks or more in the past where you felt down or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. Not for two whole weeks, anyway. I mean, I guess I lost interest in my bowling league a few years back, but that wasn't like a... a mood thing. That was just because it got way too intense. \n\nWe used to play at this alley right down the street from the casino, and my buddy Dave—the guy with the rooster alarm I mentioned earlier—he bought this custom, neon green bowling ball. I swear it looked like a radioactive melon. He insisted on polishing it with this special microfiber cloth between every single frame. It took forever. And we were playing against this team of retired blackjack dealers, who are honestly just ruthless people. They count pins like they count cards. One of them, this old guy Sal, he had this weird superstition where he had to eat exactly three pretzel bites before his turn. Not two, not four. Three. Always dipped in that fake pump cheese. Anyway, Dave accidentally dropped his radioactive melon ball right on Sal's foot during the semi-finals. It was a total disaster. The paramedics had to come and try to navigate their stretcher through all these angry seniors holding bowling shoes. \n\nAfter that whole mess, I just kind of stopped going to the alley. But as for just feeling down and flat like this for weeks at a time for no reason? No, I haven't really had that happen before.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting two weeks or more in the past where you felt down or lost interest in things you usually enjoy?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? But yeah, like I was saying, no. I haven't really had a time where I felt this flat for two weeks straight before. \n\nTwo weeks is a weird amount of time to track, though, especially in my line of work. Working in a casino, time just sort of... evaporates. There are zero windows in the surveillance room. None on the main gaming floor either, obviously. It's a psychological trick, you know? Keep the gamblers from seeing the sun come up so they lose track of time and keep spending. They pump in oxygen, too. At least, that's what my buddy Marcus swears they do. Marcus works the high-limit craps tables. He's got this crazy conspiracy theory that the pit bosses are secretly venting pure oxygen through the AC grates to keep the whales awake. \n\nHe actually bought this little handheld air quality monitor off the internet last year just to prove it. He was sneaking it onto the floor in his vest pocket, acting like some kind of undercover spy. The problem is, he forgot to turn off the warning alarm on the device. So right in the middle of this massive run—some guy was rolling for a twenty-thousand-dollar pot—Marcus's pocket starts shrieking like a smoke detector. My guys on the floor had to basically tackle him because they thought someone had rigged a device or something. It was an absolute circus. He almost got fired, but the casino manager just thought he was an idiot and suspended him for a week instead. \n\nBut anyway... what were we talking about? Oh, right, feeling down in the past. Yeah, no, never for two whole weeks like this. Just this time."
